U^{n,R,0}_{\leq n+1,\bullet}\cong h^{\mathcal{C}}_{M_{n+1}(X)}
. Identifying
U^{n,R,1}_{\leq n+1,\bullet}
as an arrow
U^{n,R,1,\prime}_{\leq n+1,\bullet}:\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n+1}:\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n+1}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})_{/G}
, we have that
U^{n,R,1,\prime}_{\leq n+1,\bullet}
may be identified as the right Kan extension of the constant diagram
\underline{\eta}:\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})_{/G}
along
\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n+1}
. Noting that
\eta
is
\infty
-connective, it follows from
[
7
]
6.5.3.5 that the canonical map
\gamma^{\prime}:\underline{\eta}\rightarrow U^{n,R,1,\prime}_{\leq n+1,\bullet}
of diagrams
\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n+1}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})_{/G}
exhibiting
U^{n,R,1,\prime}_{\leq n+1,\bullet}
as the
n
-coskeleton of
\underline{\eta}
satsifies the property that the edge
\gamma^{\prime}_{n+1}:\eta\rightarrow U^{n,R,1,\prime}_{\leq n+1,n+1}
is an effective epimorphism in
\textnormal{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})_{/G}
.

Applying
3.3.5
, we deduce that extending
U_{\leq n,\bullet}:(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}))
to a map
(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n+1})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}))
is equivalent to giving a commutative square

A
h^{\mathcal{C}}_{M_{n+1}(X)}
B
F^{\prime}_{n+1}

in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
, where
F^{\prime}_{n+1}:=U^{n,R,1}_{\leq n+1,n+1}
, and the right vertical map is induced by the image of
[n]\in(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj},\leq n})^{\triangleright}
under
U_{\leq n,\bullet}
. Writing
\gamma_{n+1}:F\rightarrow F^{\prime}_{n+1}
for the image of
\gamma^{\prime}_{n+1}
under the projection
\textnormal{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})_{/G}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})
, we have a cospan in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
of the form
F\xrightarrow{\gamma^{\prime}_{n+1}}F^{\prime}_{n+1}\leftarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{M_{n+1}(X)}
, where

is an effective epimorphism in
\textnormal{Shv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})
. Arguing as in the base case, there exists some (

-covering
\{V_{j}\rightarrow M_{n+1}(X)\}_{j\in J}
such that the cospan
F\xrightarrow{\gamma^{\prime}_{n+1}}F^{\prime}_{n+1}\leftarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{M_{n+1}(X)}
may be completed to a commutative square

F

in

. Choosing

to be the map induced by said square (in the sense of

) completes our inductive step.

\square

4
Sheaves on Topoi

4.1
Truncated Sheaves on Topoi

The main result of this section is the following:

Proposition 4.1.1
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}\cup\{\infty\}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Let
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
be an integer such that
m\leq n
if
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, let
\mathcal{D}
be an
(m,1)
-category, and let
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a map. Then:

(1)

F
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
if and only if
F
preserves small limits.

(2)

F
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
if and only if
F
factors as
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{(\tau_{\leq m-1})^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{\leq m-1}}\mathcal{D}
, where
F_{\leq m-1}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{X}
.

Remark 4.1.2
.

In the case that
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 2}
, claim (1) of
4.1.1
is mathematical folklore, and the case that
n=1
is a classical result (
[
5
]
C.2.2.7). In addition, if
\mathcal{D}
is locally small, (1) implies (2) (see
4.1.9
).

Our proof of
4.1.1
will require several preliminaries.

Definition 4.1.3 (HTT 6.4.1.5)
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
be an integer. We call an
(n,1)
-category
\mathcal{X}
an
(n,1)
-topos
if any of the following equivalent definitions are met:

(1)

There exists a small finite complete
(n,1)
-category
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a Grothendieck topology
J
, and an equivalence
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})\cong\mathcal{X}
, where
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})\subset\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
is the full subcategory consisting of those
J
-sheaves
F:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
which take values in
\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S}
.

(1^{\prime})

There exists a small finite complete
(n,1)
-category
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a subcanonical Grothendieck topology
J
, and an equivalence
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})\cong\mathcal{X}
, where
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})\subset\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
is the full subcategory consisting of those
J
-sheaves
F:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
which take values in
\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S}
.

(2)

There exists an
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{Y}
and an equivalence
\mathcal{X}\cong\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{Y}
.

(3)

\mathcal{X}
satisfies the following:

(a)

\mathcal{X}
is a presentable
(n,1)
-category

(b)

(Small) colimits in
\mathcal{X}
are universal

(c)

Pairwise coproducts in
\mathcal{X}
are disjoint.

(d)

For every groupoid object
U:\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
satisfying the property that the canonical map
U(d_{0},d_{1}):U_{1}\rightarrow U_{0}\times U_{0}
is
(n-2)
-truncated,
U
is effective.

We first wish to relate effective epimorphisms in an
(n,1)
-topos
\mathcal{X}
for
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
to effective epimorphisms in an
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{Y}
satisfying
\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{Y}\cong\mathcal{X}
(see
4.1.6
). To do so, we first verify that every
(n,1)
-topos is a semitopos, as alluded to in the introduction of
[
7
]
6.2.3:

Lemma 4.1.4
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos for
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}\cup\{\infty\}
. Then,
\mathcal{X}
is a semitopos (in the sense of
[
7
]
6.2.3.1).

Proof.

The case for
n=\infty
is given by
[
7
]
6.2.3.2, so we instead treat the case that
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. It suffices to verify that for any map
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{X}
, the underlying simplicial object of the Čech nerve of
f
is effective. Applying property (d) of characterization (3) of
4.1.3
, it suffices to show that for all maps
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{X}
, the map
X\times_{Y}X\xrightarrow{\textnormal{pr}_{1},\textnormal{pr}_{2}}X\times X
is
(n-2)
-truncated. That is, we wish to show that for all
A\in\mathcal{X}
the map
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{X}}(A,X)\times_{\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{X}}(A,Y)}\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{X}}(A,X)\rightarrow\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{X}}(A,X)\times\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{X}}(A,X)
is an
(n-2)
-truncated map of spaces. Noting that
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{X}}(A,Y)
is
(n-1)
-truncated (since
\mathcal{X}
is an
(n,1)
-category), it suffices to show that whenever
g:U\rightarrow V
is a map of spaces with
V

(n-1)
-truncated, the map
U\times_{V}U\rightarrow U\times U
is
(n-2)
-truncated. Applying
[
10
]
056B, we deduce that this is equivalent to asking that for all pairs
a,b\in U
, the homotopy fibre of
U\times_{V}U\rightarrow U\times U
over
(a,b)
is
(n-2)
-truncated. Our claim now follows from the fact that said homotopy fibre may be identified as the
V
-mapping space
\textnormal{Hom}_{V}(g(a),g(b))
.

\square

Corollary 4.1.5
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}\cup\infty
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Then, a map
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{X}
is an effective epimorphism if and only if the map
\tau_{\leq 0}f:\tau_{\leq 0}X\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}Y
is an effective epimorphism in the ordinary topos
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

The case for
n=\infty
is treated in
[
4
]
3.12, so it suffices to prove our claim for
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. Choose an
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{Y}
, and an equivalence
i:\mathcal{X}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{Y}\subset\mathcal{Y}
. Let
f:U\rightarrow V
be a map in
\mathcal{X}
such that
\tau_{\leq 0}f
is an effective epimorphism in
\mathcal{X}^{\heartsuit}
. Since the map
i
induces an equivalence
\mathcal{X}^{\heartsuit}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{Y}^{\heartsuit}
, this implies that
i(f)
is an effective epimorphism in
\mathcal{Y}
. Since
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
preserves Čech nerves and is fully faithful, this immediately implies that
f
is an effective epimorphism in
\mathcal{X}
.

To show the converse, we first recall that every
(n,1)
-topos is a semitopos, in the sense of
[
7
]
6.2.3.1 (
4.1.4
). Invoking
[
7
]
6.2.3.10, it now suffices to show that, whenever a map
f:U\rightarrow V
in
\mathcal{X}
is an effective epimorphism in
\mathcal{X}
, the induced map
\tau_{\leq 0}f^{*}:\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}V)\rightarrow\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}U)
is injective. Let
F_{\bullet}:(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be the Čech nerve of
f
, noting that the composition
(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\heartsuit}
is a colimit diagram. Combining
4.1.4
with
[
7
]
6.2.3.13, we have that
\coprod_{n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}\tau_{\leq 0}F_{n}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}V
is an effective epimorphism in
\mathcal{X}^{\heartsuit}
. In particular, this implies that the map
\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}V)\rightarrow\textnormal{Sub}(\coprod_{n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}\tau_{\leq 0}F_{n})\cong\prod_{n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}F_{n})
is injective (see
[
7
]
6.2.3.9). Since each of the induced maps
\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}V)\rightarrow\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}F_{n})
factors through
\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}V)\rightarrow\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}U)=\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}F_{0})
, it follows that
\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}V)\rightarrow\textnormal{Sub}(\tau_{\leq 0}U)
is injective, proving our claim.

\square

Corollary 4.1.6
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and let
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq n}\cup\{\infty\}
. Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(m,1)
-topos. Then, a map
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{X}
is an effective epimorphism if and only if
\tau_{\leq n-1}f
is an effective epimorphism in the
(n,1)
-topos
\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{X}
.

We may now use
4.1.6
to deduce the following component of
4.1.1
(2):

Corollary 4.1.7
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(\infty,1)
-topos, let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, and let
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a
\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})
-sheaf. Then,
F_{n}:=F|_{(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{opp}}}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X}
, and
F
factors as
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{(\tau_{\leq n})^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{n}}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
.

Proof.

Let
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a
\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})
-sheaf. It follows from
4.1.5
that
\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}
preserves effective epimorphisms: since this is also left exact, we observe that whenever the composition
(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{opp}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
preserves small products, said composition is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X}
(see
3.3.11
). Again applying
3.3.11
, we see that the left exact map
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
preserves finite limits, effective epimorphisms, and small coproducts. In particular, this is a map of
\infty
-pretopoi, in the sense of
[
9
]
A.6.4.1. It now follows from
[
9
]
A.6.7.1 that for all
X\in\mathcal{X}
and
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, the map
h^{\mathcal{X}}_{X}\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{X}}_{\tau_{\leq n}X}
(in
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
) exhibits
h^{\mathcal{X}}_{\tau_{\leq n}X}
as the
n
-truncation of
h^{\mathcal{X}}_{X}
. In particular, the
n
-truncated
\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})
-sheaf
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
factors as
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{n}}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
. Since
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{opp}}
is essentially surjective and preserves small products, it follows that
F_{n}
also preserves small products, as desired.

\square

Our proof of the remainder of
4.1.1
will rely on establishing the following two facts:

(1)

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and let
\mathcal{C}
be an
(n,1)
-category equipped with a subcanonical Grothendieck topology
J
. Then, for all complete
(n,1)
-categories
\mathcal{D}
, a map
F:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
preserves small limits if and only if
F
is right Kan extended from a
J
-sheaf
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
(
4.1.10
).

(2)

Under the assumptions of
(1)
, a map
F:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})
if and only if
F
is right Kan extended from a
J
-sheaf
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
(this may be deduced from
4.1.14
).

We now prove these claims in order.

Lemma 4.1.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
(\infty,1)
-category equipped with a subcanonical topology
J
, and let
\mathcal{D}
be a complete
(\infty,1)
-category. Let
F:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a map, write
F_{*}:\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
for the right Kan extension of
F
along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}
, and write
F^{\prime}_{*}:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
for the right Kan extension of
F
along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}
. Then, the following are equivalent:

(1)

F:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a
J
-sheaf, in the sense of
2.1.7
.

(2)

For all
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
J
-sieves
\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{/X}
, the monomorphism
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
corresponding to
\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{/X}
under the equivalence of
[
7
]
6.2.2.5 is carried to an isomorphism by
(F_{*}^{\prime})^{\textnormal{opp}}
.

(3)

F^{\prime}_{*}
factors as
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{L^{\textnormal{opp}}}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{*}}\mathcal{D}
, where
L:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
is left adjoint to the inclusion.

(4)

F_{*}
preserves small limits.

Proof.

Consider the map
F^{\prime}_{*}:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
. Let
X\in\mathcal{C}
, let
\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{/X}
be a
J
-sieve on
X
, and consider the corresponding monomorphism
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
. Noting
S
is the colimit of the composition
\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
, the map
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
is induced by the composition
(\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{C}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
. Thus, the composition
(\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{C}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{(F_{*})^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}}
is a colimit diagram if and only if
(F_{*}^{\prime})^{\textnormal{opp}}
carries
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
to an isomorphism. Since
(\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{C}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{(F_{*})^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}}
agrees with the composition
(\mathcal{C}^{0}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow(\mathcal{C}_{/X})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{F^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}}
, we see that (1) and (2) are equivalent.

Next, assume that (2) holds, and let
\mu:\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
be the unit map exhibiting
L
as left adjoint to the inclusion. Since
F_{*}
preserves small limits, it follows from
2.1.4
that
F_{*}
carries every
L^{\textnormal{opp}}
-equivalence to an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}
. Noting also that
F_{*}^{\prime}
restricts to
F_{*}
(up to isomorphism), the composition
\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{(F^{\prime}_{*})^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}}
induces a natural isomorphism from the composition
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{L^{\textnormal{opp}}}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{*}}\mathcal{D}
to
F^{\prime}_{*}
, implying (3). Since (3) automatically implies (2), we have that (2) and (3) are equivalent.

Finally, suppose that (3) is satisfied. Since
F_{*}
preserves small limits, and
L^{\textnormal{opp}}
is essentially surjective and preserves all existing limits in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}
, (4) immediately follows. If (4) holds, the composition
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{L^{\textnormal{opp}}}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{*}}\mathcal{D}
preserves small limits, and as such, is the right Kan extension of the map
F_{0}:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
given by the composition
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{L^{\textnormal{opp}}}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{*}}\mathcal{D}
along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}
. Since
J
is subcanonical we may identify
F_{0}
as
F
, proving (3).

\square

Lemma 4.1.9
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a locally small
(\infty,1)
-category, and let
L:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
be a localization at a (possibly large) set of arrows
W
in
\mathcal{E}
. Then, a map
F:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
factors as
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{L}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
if and only if, for each
X\in\mathcal{D}
, the composition
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{D}\xrightarrow{\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(X,-)}\mathcal{S}
factors as
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{L}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(X,-)
are jointly conservative.

\square

Corollary 4.1.10
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq-1}
, let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
(n,1)
-category equipped with a subcanonical topology
J
, and let
\mathcal{D}
be a complete
(n,1)
-category. Then, a map
F:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
preserves small limits if and only if
F:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is the right Kan extension of a
J
-sheaf
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}
.

Proof.

Changing universe if necessary, without loss of generality we may assume that
\mathcal{D}
admits a Yoneda embedding
h^{\mathcal{D}}_{\bullet}:\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
. Combining
4.1.8
with
4.1.9
, it now suffices to prove our claim in the case that
\mathcal{D}=\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
.

To begin, let
F:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a
J
-sheaf, noting that
h_{F}:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
may be identified as the right Kan extension of
F
along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}
(
4.1.8
). Noting that
h_{F}:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
factors as
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{h_{F}}\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
, it follows from
5.2.5
that the composition
\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{h_{F}}\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
preserves small limits. It now follows from
5.2.1
(2) implies that the composition
\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}\hookrightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{h_{F}}\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
preserves small limits, which in turn implies that the restriction
h_{F}|_{(\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}}
preserves small limits. This direction of our claim now follows from the fact that
h_{F}|_{(\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}}
may be identified as the right Kan extension of
F:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}
.

For the converse, let
G:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a map, which we right Kan extend along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}
to an arrow
G^{\prime}:(\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
. It follows that the composition
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C}))^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{G^{\prime}}\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
, so the fact that
G
is a
J
-sheaf now follows from
4.1.8
.

\square

We will not need the following, though it is an immediate consequence of
4.1.10
:

Corollary 4.1.11
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq-1}
, and let
\mathcal{D}
be a small
(n,1)
-category. Then,
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S})
satisfies the following universal property: for all cocomplete
(n,1)
-categories
\mathcal{E}
, pulling-back along
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S})
induces an equivalence of
(n,1)
-categories
\textnormal{Fun}^{co\ell im}(\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S}),\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{E})
. Moreover, a map
G:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
preserves small colimits if and only if
G
is left Kan extended from
\mathcal{D}
.

A key part of our proof of
4.1.1
relies on the following theorem (
[
1
]
A.6):

Theorem 4.1.12 ([Aoki23] A.6)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
(\infty,1)
-category equipped with a topology
J
, and let
F:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a fully faithful
(\infty,1)
-functor. Suppose that, for each
X\in\mathcal{C}
, there exists a (small) set of morphisms
\{F(B_{i})\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{C}
generating a
J
-sieve on
X
, where each
B_{i}
is in
\mathcal{B}
. Equip
\mathcal{B}
with the topology
J_{\mathcal{B}}
, where for each
U\in\mathcal{B}
, the collection of
J_{\mathcal{B}}
-sieves on
U
is defined as those sieves
\mathcal{B}^{0}_{/U}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}_{/U}
on
U
where the fully faithful map
\mathcal{B}^{0}_{/U}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{/F(U)}
generates a
J
-sieve on
U\in\mathcal{C}
. Then, a map
H:\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
is a
J
-hypersheaf if and only if
H
is the right Kan extension of a
J_{\mathcal{B}}
-hypersheaf on
\mathcal{B}
along
F
.

Definition 4.1.13
.

Under the notation of
4.1.12
, we will say that the pair
(\mathcal{B},J_{\mathcal{B}})
is a
basis
for the pair
(\mathcal{C},J)
.

Proposition 4.1.14
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and let
\mathcal{C}
be an
(n,1)
-category equipped with a subcanonical topology
J
. Then,
(\mathcal{C},J)
is a basis for
(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C}an(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})))
.

Proof.

Since for all
F\in\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})
we may choose a small set
I
of objects
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{C}
such that there exists an effective epimorphism
\coprod_{i\in I}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X_{i}}\rightarrow F
, it only remains to show that the topology on
\mathcal{C}
induced by the canonical topology on
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})
(in the sense of
4.1.12
) is equal to
J
.

Let
X\in\mathcal{C}
, and let
\{f_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
be a (small) set of arrows in
\mathcal{C}
. Write
\coprod_{i\in I}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{e}S\xrightarrow{m}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
for the epi-mono factorization in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S})
of the induced map
\coprod_{i\in I}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{U_{i}}\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
, and denote with
L:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{n-1}^{J}(\mathcal{C})
the
J
-sheafification functor. Since
L
preserves both effective epimorphisms and monomorphisms, we deduce there exists an epi-mono factorization
\coprod_{i\in I}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}}L(S)\xrightarrow{m^{\prime}}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
of
\coprod_{i\in I}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{U_{i}}\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
in
\textnormal{Shv}_{n-1}^{J}(\mathcal{C})
, where
e^{\prime}\cong L(e)
and
m^{\prime}\cong L(m)
in the arrow category of
\textnormal{Shv}_{n-1}^{J}(\mathcal{C})
. This implies that
\coprod_{i\in I}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{U_{i}}\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
is an effective epimorphism in
\textnormal{Shv}_{n-1}^{J}(\mathcal{C})
precisely when
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
is an
L
-equivalence. It now follows from
[
7
]
6.2.2.16 that the map
\coprod_{i\in I}h^{\mathcal{C}}_{U_{i}}\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
is an effective epimorphism in
\textnormal{Shv}_{n-1}^{J}(\mathcal{C})
if and only if the set of arrows
\{f_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
generates a
J
-covering sieve on
X\in\mathcal{C}
, completing the proof of our claim.

\square

Remark 4.1.15
.

The argument given in
4.1.14
can be easily adapted to show the following:

(i)

For all
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq n}
,
(\mathcal{C},J)
is a basis for
(\textnormal{Shv}_{m-1}^{J}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C}an(\textnormal{Shv}_{m-1}^{J}(\mathcal{C})))
.

(ii)

For any small
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{D}
equipped with a topology
K
, the pair
(\mathcal{D},K)
is a basis for
(\textnormal{Shv}_{K}(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{C}an(\textnormal{Shv}_{K}(\mathcal{D})))
.

We may now prove
4.1.1
(1):

Corollary 4.1.16
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Let
\mathcal{D}
be an
(n,1)
-category. Then, a map
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
preserves small limits if and only if
F
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

Just as in the proof of
4.1.10
, it now suffices to prove that a map
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
precisely when
F
preserves small limits. Invoking
3.3.11
, we deduce that whenever
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
preserves small limits,
F
is a
\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})
-sheaf, proving one direction of our claim.

We now prove the converse. Choose some small
(n,1)
-category
\mathcal{C}
, a Grothendieck topology
J
on
\mathcal{C}
, and an equivalence
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{X}
. Since every sheaf
F:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
for the effective epimorphism topology on
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})
is an
(n-1)
-truncated object of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C}))}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C}))
, each is hypercomplete (see
[
7
]
6.5.2.9). Combining
4.1.12
with
4.1.14
, it follows that
F
is the right Kan extension of a
J
-sheaf along the map
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{n-1}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}
, so our result now follows from
4.1.10
.

\square

We conclude this section by proving
4.1.1
(2):

Corollary 4.1.17
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}\cup\{\infty\}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Let
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
be an integer such that
m\leq n
if
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. Then, for all
(m,1)
-categories
\mathcal{D}
, a map
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
if and only if
F
factors as

\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{(\tau_{\leq m-1})^{\textnormal{opp}}}\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{m-1}}\mathcal{D},

where
F_{m-1}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

We first prove our claim in the case that
n=\infty
. To begin, let
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
. Combining
4.1.7
with
4.1.9
, we deduce that
F
factors as
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{(\tau_{\leq m-1})^{\textnormal{opp}}}\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{m-1}}\mathcal{D},
where
F_{m-1}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
. The converse follows immediately from combining
4.1.16
with
3.3.1
.

We now prove our claim in the case that
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. Suppose again that
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
. Choose some
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{Y}
, and an equivalence
\mathcal{X}\cong\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{Y}
. Since the composition
\mathcal{Y}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{D}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{Y}
, it follows from the above that the composition
\mathcal{Y}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{D}
factors as
\mathcal{Y}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{Y})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}
where
F^{\prime}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{Y}
. It now follows that
F
factors as
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{D}
, where
F^{\prime\prime}
is identified with
F^{\prime}
under the equivalence
\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{Y}\cong\tau_{\leq m-1}\mathcal{X}
. The converse follows immediately from
4.1.16
.

\square

4.2
Sheaves on Topoi

The main result of this section is the following theorem:

Theorem 4.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(\infty,1)
-topos, and write
\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})
for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
. Then:

(1)

The inclusion
\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{X})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
factors through
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})\subset\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
. Moreover, the left adjoint
C:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{X})
induced by HTT 6.3.5.28 is a cotopological localization (in our larger universe).

(2)

For all
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, pulling-back along the localization
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X}
yields a categorical equivalence
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X})\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
, where
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X})
is the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
spanned by those maps
F:\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
which preserve small limits.

(3)

Pulling-back along
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{hyp}}
yields a categorical equivalence
\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{hyp}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})^{\textnormal{hyp}}
.

Remark 4.2.2
.

In light of
(1)
, the respective classifications of the truncated and hypercomplete objects of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
given by
(2)
and
(3)
also classify the analogous data for
\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{X})
.

Before proving
4.2.1
, we discuss several consequences:

Corollary 4.2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a hypercomplete
(\infty,1)
-topos. Then, a map
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
is a hypersheaf with respect to the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
if and only if
F
is representable.

This is the higher analogue of the following, which may be deduced from
4.1.1
:

Corollary 4.2.4
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Then, a map
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S}
is a sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
if and only if
F
is representable.

We again note that the case of
4.2.4
for
n=1
is a classical result (see
[
5
]
C.2.2.7). In the case that
\mathcal{X}
is not hypercomplete, we have the following connection to
4.2.4
:

Corollary 4.2.5
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}\cup\{\infty\}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Let
Y\in\mathcal{X}
, and let
\mathcal{X}^{0}_{/Y}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{/Y}
be a sieve on
Y
such that every representable functor in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
is local with respect to the induced monomorphism
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{X}}_{Y}
. Then,
\mathcal{X}^{0}_{/Y}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{/Y}
is a sieve for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

We first show this in the case that
n=\infty
. Write
L:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{X})
be the map left adjoint to the inclusion. Applying
[
7
]
6.3.5.28, we deduce that whenever
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
factors as
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
, so too does
L(F):\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
. Next, let
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{X}}_{Y}
be a monomorphism in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
such that every representable functor is
m
-local. Since
m
is a monomorphism we may identify
m
as an edge
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{X}}_{Y}
in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
, and it immediately follows that
m
is an
L^{\prime}
-equivalence. Since
L^{\prime}
is the restriction of
L
(in the appropriate sense), it follows that
m
is an
L^{\prime}
-equivalence. Writing
T:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
for the sheafification functor, combining
[
7
]
6.2.2.16,
[
7
]
6.5.2.16 and
4.2.1
implies that
m
is also a
T
-equivalence, proving this part of our claim.

We now prove our claim in the case that
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. Choose some
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{Y}
, and an equivalence
\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{Y}\cong\mathcal{X}
. Let
G:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{Y}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{Y})
be left adjoint to the inclusion. Noting now that the arrow
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{Y}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
is fully faithful and write
G^{\prime}:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{Y})
for the map induced by the restriction of
G
. Since
G
is left exact,
G^{\prime}
restricts to an arrow
G^{\prime\prime}:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n-1}\textnormal{Shv}_{\widehat{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathcal{Y})
. It follows from
4.2.1
(2) that
G^{\prime\prime}
is isomorphic to the map
F:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}\vphantom{}^{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}_{n-1}(\mathcal{X})
left adjoint to the inclusion, and it now follows from
[
7
]
6.3.5.28 that
F
restricts (in the appropriate sense) to a map
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}\vphantom{}_{n-1}^{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})\cong\mathcal{X}
left adjoint to the Yoneda embedding. Our claim now follows from a similar logic to that used in the
n=\infty
case above.

\square

Corollary
4.2.5
immediately implies the following:

Corollary 4.2.6
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}\cup\{\infty\}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Then, there exists a unique finest Grothendieck topology
J
on
\mathcal{X}
satisfying the property that every representable functor
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
is a
J
-sheaf. Moreover, this is the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
.

Corollary 4.2.7
.

Choose some integer
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos. Then, the sheafification functor
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}\vphantom{}^{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}_{n-1}(\mathcal{X})
restricts to a left exact map
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}\vphantom{}_{n-1}^{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})\cong\mathcal{X}
left adjoint to the Yoneda embedding.

Proof.

See the proof of
4.2.5
.

\square

We also have the following the characterization of geometric morphisms of
(n,1)
-topoi:

Corollary 4.2.8
.

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}\cup\{\infty\}
, and let
m\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
such that
m\leq n
if
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(n,1)
-topos, and let
\mathcal{D}
be an
(m,1)
-category. Let
F:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be an
(\infty,1)
-functor which preserves pullbacks and small coproducts. Then,
F
preserves small colimits if and only if
F
preserves effective epimorphisms.

We now prove
4.2.1
. Noting that the factorization of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
described in (1) is implied by
3.3.1
, we may deduce the remainder of (1) from
4.1.1
:

Proof.

(
4.2.1
(1).) Write
i:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
for the Yoneda embedding. Let
K
be a small simplicial set, let
F:K\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a diagram, and let
\overline{F}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a diagram which exhibits some
X\in\mathcal{X}
as the colimit of
F
. Writing
\overline{i\circ F}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
for a map which exhibits some
G\in\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
as the colimit of
i\circ F
, we have a canonical morphism
j:G\rightarrow i(X)
. In order to prove our claim, it suffices to show that
j
is
\infty
-connective. Applying
[
7
]
6.5.1.14, it suffices to show that for all
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
U\in\tau_{\leq n}(\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(X)})\subseteq\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(X)}
,
U
is local with respect to the map
j^{\prime}:j\rightarrow\textnormal{id}_{i(X)}
, where
j^{\prime}
denotes the right degenerate
2
-simplex of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
corresponding to
j
. Fix such an
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and such a
U\in\tau_{\leq n}(\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(X)})
. Next, identify
j:G\rightarrow i(X)
as an edge
K\star\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{(i\circ F)/}
. Noting that
\Delta^{1}
is weakly contractible,
[
10
]
0198 implies that
\Delta^{1}\hookrightarrow K\star\Delta^{1}
is right anodyne. In particular, there exists an extension
J:K\star\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(X)}
of
j^{\prime}:\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(\mathcal{X})}
along
\Delta^{1}\hookrightarrow K\star\Delta^{1}
such that the composition
K\star\Delta^{1}\xrightarrow{J}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(\mathcal{X})}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
agrees with the edge of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{(i\circ F)/}
induced by
j:G\rightarrow i(X)
. In order to prove our claim, it now suffices to show that the composition

\Delta^{1}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{1}\star(K^{\textnormal{opp}})=(\Delta^{1})^{\textnormal{opp}}\star(K^{\textnormal{opp}})=(K\star\Delta^{1})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{J^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(\mathcal{X})})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{h_{U}}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}

is an isomorphism in
\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
. Since the composition
K^{\triangleright}=K\star\{0\}\hookrightarrow K\star\Delta^{1}\xrightarrow{J}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(\mathcal{X})}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})
is a colimit diagram, it suffices to prove that the composition

(K^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleleft}=(K\star\{1\})^{\textnormal{opp}}\hookrightarrow(K\star\Delta^{1})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{J^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(\mathcal{X})})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{h_{U}}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}},

which we will denote by
F^{\prime}:(K^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleleft}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
, is a limit diagram. Writing
\phi:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{X})_{/i(\mathcal{X})}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X}_{/X})}(\mathcal{X}_{/X})
for the categorical equivalence of
3.1.23
, it follows from
2.2.7
that
F^{\prime}
may be identified as the composition
(K^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleleft}=(K^{\triangleright})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F_{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\mathcal{X}_{/X})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{\phi(U)}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
, where
F_{X}
is a lifting of
F:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
along the cone point. Our claim now follows from
4.1.1
.

\square

Noting that
4.2.1
(2) is an immediate corollary of
4.1.1
, it only remains to prove
4.2.1
(3):

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
(\infty,1)
-topos, and let
F:\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a map which preserves small limits. Invoking
3.3.1
, it suffices to prove the following:
F
carries every
\infty
-connective morphism in
\mathcal{X}
to an isomorphism in
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
if and only if
F
, for all
\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})
-hypercoverings
X_{\bullet}:(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, the composition
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}^{\triangleleft}\xrightarrow{X_{\bullet}^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is a limit diagram.

To begin, suppose that
F
carries every
\infty
-connective morphism in
\mathcal{X}
to an isomorphism in
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
, and let
X_{\bullet}:(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a hypercovering of
X\in\mathcal{X}
for the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
. Identify
X_{\bullet}
as a map
X^{\prime}_{\bullet}:\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{/X}
, and extend
X^{\prime}_{\bullet}
to a colimit diagram
\overline{X^{\prime}_{\bullet}}:(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{/X}
, noting the cone point of of
\overline{X^{\prime}_{\bullet}}
is
\infty
-connective (
[
9
]
A.5.6.3). In particular,
\overline{X^{\prime}_{\bullet}}
is
\infty
-connective as an edge of
\mathcal{X}
(
[
7
]
6.5.1.19), so it immediately follows that the composition
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}^{\triangleleft}\xrightarrow{X_{\bullet}^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is a limit diagram.

We now prove the converse. Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be an
\infty
-connective morphism in
\mathcal{X}
, which we identify as a vertex of
\mathcal{X}_{/Y}
. Let
\underline{f}:\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{/Y}
be the constant diagram with value
f
, which we identify as an arrow
\underline{f}^{\prime}:(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
with conepoint
Y
. Applying
[
7
]
6.5.3.5 we deduce that
\underline{f}^{\prime}
is a
\mathcal{C}an(\mathcal{X})
-hypercovering of
Y
, implying that the composition
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}^{\triangleleft}\xrightarrow{(\underline{f}^{\prime})^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is a limit diagram. Since every edge of the underlying map
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is an isomorphism and
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}
is weakly contractible, it follows that the composition
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}^{\triangleleft}\xrightarrow{(\underline{f}^{\prime})^{\textnormal{opp}}}\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
carries every edge of
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}^{\triangleleft}
to an isomorphism. Our claim now follows from the fact that for each
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, the arrow
\underline{f}^{\prime}:(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
carries the
[n]\rightarrow[-1]
edge of
(\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\textnormal{inj}})^{\triangleright}
to the map
f:X\rightarrow Y
.

\square

4.3
Global Sheaves on Topoi

As discussed in
3.1.19
, the étale topology on the
(\infty,1)
-category of
(\infty,1)
-topoi,
\infty\mathcal{T}op
, is generated by a geometric site. Just as the effective epimorphism topology on an
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{X}
is a good starting point when working to identify the appropriate notion of descent on
\mathcal{X}
, the étale topology on
\infty\mathcal{T}op
is a good starting point when working to identify the appropriate notion of descent on
\infty\mathcal{T}op
. However, just as the appropriate notion of a sheaf on an
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{X}
does not in general correspond to the effective epimorphism topology on
\mathcal{X}
(though
4.2.1
establishes the fact that these notions are intimately related), the additional structure of higher category theory allows one to instead ask for a stronger notion of descent on
\infty\mathcal{T}op
than any Grothendieck topology can provide:

Definition 4.3.1 (HTT 6.3.5.19)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be an
(\infty,1)
-category, and let
F:\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a map. We call
F
a
sheaf
on
\infty\mathcal{T}op
if
F
satisfies either of the following equivalent conditions:

(1)

The restriction of
F
to
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t},\textnormal{opp}}\subset\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}}
preserves small limits.

(2)

For all
(\infty,1)
-topoi
\mathcal{X}
, the composition
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t},\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{D}
is a sheaf on
\mathcal{X}
, where
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}})^{\textnormal{opp}}
is the canonical equivalence of
[
7
]
6.3.5.10.

We will use the following relative variant of
4.3.1
:

Definition 4.3.2
.

Let
F:\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a sheaf on
\infty\mathcal{T}op
, and let
\mathcal{D}
be an
(\infty,1)
-category. Noting that
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}:=\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}\times_{\infty\mathcal{T}op}\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F}
admits small colimits which are preserved by
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}\hookrightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F}
(see
4.3.4
), we will call a map
G:(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
a
sheaf
on
\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F}
if
G
satisfies either of the following equivalent conditions:

(1)

The restriction of
G
to
(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}}
preserves small limits.

(2)

For all
(\infty,1)
-topoi
\mathcal{X}
and maps
\eta:h_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow F
, the composition
\mathcal{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{\sim}((\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F})_{/(\mathcal{X},\eta)})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{G}\mathcal{D}
is a sheaf on
\mathcal{X}
.

Remark 4.3.3
.

In an unfortunate instance of notational conflict, it is
not
true that for an
(\infty,1)
-topos
\mathcal{X}
the two
(\infty,1)
-categories
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/h_{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}}
coincide (unless
\mathcal{X}
is the inital
(\infty,1)
-topos). Instead,
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/h_{\mathcal{X}}}
may be identified as the subcategory of
\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/\mathcal{X}}
spanned by the morphisms
f_{*}:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
over
\mathcal{X}
which are themselves étale, and
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}}
is the full subcategory of
\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/\mathcal{X}}
spanned by the étale morphisms
\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
(noting
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}}
may then also be identified as a full subcategory of
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/h_{\mathcal{X}}}
).

Proposition 4.3.4
.

We work under the notation of
4.3.2
. Let
K
a small simplicial set, and let
\phi:K\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}
be a diagram. Then,
\phi
admits a colimit in
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}
. Moreover, for any extension
\overline{\phi}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}
of
\phi
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\overline{\phi}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}
is a colimit diagram.

(2)

The composition
K^{\triangleright}\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}\hookrightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F}
is a colimit diagram.

(3)

The composition
K^{\triangleright}\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}
is a colimit diagram.

(4)

The composition
K^{\triangleright}\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/F}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op
is a colimit diagram.

Proof.

Combine
[
7
]
6.3.5.13 with
3.1.20
.

\square

The main result of this section is the following:

Proposition 4.3.5
.

Let
F:\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a sheaf, and write
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})
for the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}((\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
spanned by the sheaves
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
. Writing
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}_{/F}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})
for the full subcategory spanned by the sheaves for the étale topology on
\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F}
(in the sense of
2.2.1
(1)), we then have the following:

(1)

The inclusion
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}((\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
factors as the composition
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})\hookrightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}_{/F}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}((\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
.

(2)

The inclusion
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})\hookrightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}_{/F}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})
induced by
(1)
admits a cotopologoical (in our larger universe) left adjoint
C:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}_{/F}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})
.

Moreover, the inclusion
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}((\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
identifies
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{hyp}}
with the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}((\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
spanned by those maps
G:(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
which satisfy the following two properties:

(\textnormal{i})

G
preserves small products.

(\textnormal{ii})

For all augmented semisimplicial objects
X_{\bullet}:(\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F}
satisfying the property that for each
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
the
n
th covering map
X_{n}\rightarrow M_{n}(X)
is both étale and an effective epimorphism of
(\infty,1)
-topoi, the composition
\Delta_{\textnormal{inj}}^{\triangleleft}\xrightarrow{X^{\textnormal{opp}}_{\bullet}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{G}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is a limit diagram.

Proof.

Claim
(1)
follows from
3.3.1
. Once we prove
(2)
, the component of our claim identifying the hypercomplete objects of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
will also follow from
3.3.1
(see also
3.1.19
(6)), so it only remains to
(2)
. To begin, we wish to show that the equivalence
\textnormal{Fun}((\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Fun}(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})_{/F}
restricts to an equivalence
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F}
, where
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
is the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
spanned by the sheaves. Applying
2.2.3
, it suffices to show that
E:((\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is a sheaf if and only if the right fibration given by the composition
(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F})_{/E}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/F}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op
is classified by a sheaf
E^{\prime}:\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
. This fact may be deduced from combining
4.3.4
with
3.1.20
.

In order to now prove our claim, it suffices to show that the map
L_{F}:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F}
left adjoint to the inclusion is a cotopological localization (in our larger universe). Noting that
F\in\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
is local with respect to the localization functor
L:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
induced by
(1)
, we may identify
L_{F}:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F}
as the map on slices induced by
L
. Note now the following:

(\textnormal{a})

L_{F}
is left exact if
L:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
preserves pullbacks.

(\textnormal{b})

If
L
is a localization at a (possibly large) set of arrows
S
in
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
, then
L_{F}
is a localization at
p^{-1}(S)
, where
p:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
is the projection map (
2.2.13
).

(\textnormal{c})

An edge
f:X\rightarrow Y
of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/F}
is
\infty
-connective if and only if
p(f):p(X)\rightarrow p(Y)
is
\infty
-connective (
[
7
]
6.5.1.19).

It follows that
L_{F}
is a cotopological localization whenever
L
is a cotopological localization (both in our larger universe), so we are reduced to proving that
L
is cotopological. The fact that
L
is left exact follows from combining (the proof of)
[
7
]
6.2.3.20 with
[
7
]
6.3.5.21, so it only remains to show that
L
restricts to a reflective localization at a (possibly large) set of
\infty
-connective morphisms in
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
. We will achieve this using a technique very similar to that used in the proof of
4.2.1
.

Let
K
be a small simplicial set, let
\psi:K\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}\subset\infty\mathcal{T}op
be a map, and let
\overline{\psi}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\infty\mathcal{T}op
be a diagram which exhibits
\mathcal{X}\in\infty\mathcal{T}op
as the colimit of
\psi
. Let
i:\infty\mathcal{T}op\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
denote the Yoneda embedding, and write
\overline{i\circ\psi}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
for a map which exhibits some
G\in\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
as the colimit of
i\circ\psi
, yielding a canonical morphism
j:G\rightarrow i(\mathcal{X})
. In order to prove our claim, it suffices to show that
j
is
\infty
-connective. Writing
j^{\prime}:\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(\mathcal{X})}
for the map given by the right degenerate
2
-simplex
\Delta^{2}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
corresponding to
j
, it suffices to prove that for all
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
n
-truncated
U\in\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(X)}
,
U
is local with respect to
j^{\prime}
(
[
7
]
6.5.1.14). Fix such an
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
U\in\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(X)}
. Identifying
j
as an edge
K\star\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
of
\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{i\circ\psi/}
, and observing that the inclusion
\Delta^{1}\hookrightarrow K\star\Delta^{1}
is right anodyne (
[
10
]
0198), the map
K\star\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)
admits a lift to an arrow
J:K\star\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(X)}
such that the composition
\Delta^{1}\hookrightarrow K\star\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(X)}
is precisely
j^{\prime}
. Noting that the composition
K^{\triangleright}\xrightarrow{\sim}K\star\{0\}\hookrightarrow K\star\Delta^{1}\xrightarrow{J}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(X)}
is a colimit diagram, in order to prove our claim it suffices to prove that the composition

(K^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleleft}\xrightarrow{\sim}(K\star\{1\})^{\textnormal{opp}}\hookrightarrow(K\star\Delta^{1})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{J^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(X)})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{h_{U}}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}},

which we will denote by
\psi^{\prime}
, is a limit diagram. Write
\phi:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op)_{/i(\mathcal{X})}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}_{/\mathcal{X}}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/\mathcal{X}})
for the categorical equivalence induced by
2.2.1
, and let
\psi_{\mathcal{X}}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}})_{/\mathcal{X}}
be a lifting of
\phi
along the cone point of
K^{\triangleright}
. It now follows from
2.2.7
that
\psi^{\prime}
may be identified as the composition
(K^{\textnormal{opp}})^{\triangleleft}=(K^{\triangleright})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{\psi_{X}^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}})^{\textnormal{opp}}\hookrightarrow(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/\mathcal{X}})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{\phi(U)}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
. Since the composition
(\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}})^{\textnormal{opp}}\hookrightarrow(\infty\mathcal{T}op_{/\mathcal{X}})^{\textnormal{opp}}\xrightarrow{\phi(U)}\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is an
n
-truncated sheaf for the effective epimorphism topology on
\infty\mathcal{T}op^{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}_{/\mathcal{X}}\cong\mathcal{X}
(
3.3.11
), it now follows from
4.2.1
that
\psi^{\prime}
is a limit diagram, as desired.

\square

5
Appendix

5.1
Subcategories of Modules

The goal of this section is to prove the following, which we used to prove
3.2.2
:

Proposition 5.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
be a monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category satisfying the property that
\mathcal{C}
admits geometric realizations and the tensor product
\otimes:\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
preserves geometric realizations separately in each variable. Let
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
be a reflective subcategory such that the localization functor
L:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is compatible with the monoidal structure on
\mathcal{C}
, and let
A\in\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{C})
. Then:

(1)

The map
L^{\prime}:\textnormal{LMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
induced by post-composition with
L^{\otimes}
is a reflective localization.

(2)

An object
M\in\textnormal{LMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is
L^{\prime}
-local if and only if the underlying
\mathcal{C}
-object of
M
is contained in the full subcategory
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
.

Our proof of
5.1.1
will require some preliminaries.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
be monoidal
(\infty,1)
-categories whose monoidal structures are each compatible with
\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}
-indexed colimits. Let
F^{\otimes}:\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
be a monoidal
(\infty,1)
-functor such that
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
preserves geometric realizations. Then, the square

\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{C})}\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{C})
\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{D})\times_{\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{D})}\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{D})
\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{C})
\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{D})
F^{\otimes}\circ
F^{\otimes}\circ

commutes up to homotopy, where the vertical maps are given by the corresponding relative tensor product functors.

Proof.

Combine
[
8
]
4.4.2.8,
[
8
]
4.4.3.2, and
[
8
]
4.4.3.9.

\square

Corollary 5.1.3
.

Under the same assumptions as
5.1.2
, write
p:\textnormal{LMod}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{C})
and
q:\textnormal{LMod}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{D})
for the forgetful functors. Then, the map
\textnormal{LMod}_{F}(\cdot):\textnormal{LMod}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}(\mathcal{D})
given by post-composition with
F^{\otimes}
satisfies the following two properties:

(1)

\textnormal{LMod}_{F}(\cdot)
carries
p
-cartesian arrows to
q
-cartesian arrows.

(2)

\textnormal{LMod}_{F}(\cdot)
carries
p
-cocartesian arrows to
q
-cocartesian arrows.

Proof.

Claim
(1)
is true in general without any assumptions about the existence of certain geometric realizations, and may be deduced from combining
[
8
]
4.2.3.2 with the fact that the compositions
\textnormal{LMod}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\textnormal{LMod}_{F}(\cdot)}\textnormal{LMod}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
and
\textnormal{LMod}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{D}
agree.

Noting that a similar proof to
(1)
shows that
\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{BMod}(\mathcal{D})
carries arrows cartesian over
\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{C})\times\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{C})
to arrows cartesian over
\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{D})\times\textnormal{Alg}(\mathcal{D})
, claim
(2)
follows from combining
5.1.2
with the respective contructions of
p
-cocartesian arrows and
q
-cocartesian arrows given in the proof of
[
8
]
4.6.2.17.

\square

Proof.

(
5.1.1
.) Acknowledging the slight abuse of notation, we will prove the analogous claim for the map
\textnormal{LMod}^{\mathbb{A}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{\mathbb{A}_{\infty}}_{\textnormal{Alg}_{L}(A)}(\mathcal{D})
, where we identify
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
with cocartesian fibrations
P:\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}
and
Q:\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}
, respectively. Consider now the counit map
\mu:\Delta^{1}\times\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
, which is a natural isomorphism from
L^{\otimes}\circ i
to
\textnormal{id}_{\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}}
, where
i:\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
is the inclusion. In particular, since the only isomorphisms in
\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}
are the identity morphisms, the map
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}})
carries
\mu
to the identity map
\textnormal{id}_{Q}
. Consider now the edge of
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes},\textnormal{Assoc}^{\otimes})\times_{\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\textnormal{Assoc}^{\otimes})}\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes})
given by
(\textnormal{id}_{P},\mu)
, which is an isomorphism from
(P,L^{\otimes}|_{\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}})
to
(P,\textnormal{id}_{\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}})
. Next, combine
[
10
]
01TB with (the opposite of)
[
10
]
039R to deduce that
L^{\otimes}
is
Q
-right Kan extended from
\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
. Since the map
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes},\textnormal{Assoc}^{\otimes})\times_{\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\textnormal{Assoc}^{\otimes})}\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes})
carries
L^{\otimes}
to
(P,L^{\otimes}|_{\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}})
, it follows from
[
10
]
030R that there exists an arrow
L^{\prime,\otimes}:\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
isomorphic to
L^{\otimes}
, such that
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes},\textnormal{Assoc}^{\otimes})\times_{\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\textnormal{Assoc}^{\otimes})}\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes})
carries
L^{\prime,\otimes}
to
(P,\textnormal{id}_{\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}})
. In particular,
L^{\prime,\otimes}
is a monoidal
(\infty,1)
-functor, and satisfies the property that
L^{\prime,\otimes}|_{\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}}=\textnormal{id}_{\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}}
(as a map of simplicial sets). For the remainder of this proof, we will suppose that
L^{\otimes}:\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
is equal (as a map of simplicial sets) to
L^{\prime,\otimes}
.

Consider now the reflective localization
L^{\prime\prime}:\textnormal{Alg}_{/\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{Alg}_{/\Delta^{\textnormal{opp}}}(\mathcal{D})
given by post-composition with
L^{\otimes}
. Noting that the map
\textnormal{Hom}(A,L(A))\rightarrow\textnormal{Hom}(L(A),L(A))
is a homotopy equivalence, we may choose some
f:A\rightarrow L(A)
which is carried to
\textnormal{id}_{L(A)}
(up to equivalence). Next, combining
5.1.3
with
[
10
]
05J5 implies that the compositions
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{L(A)\otimes_{A}(\cdot)}\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
and
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{L(A)\otimes_{L(A)}(\cdot)}\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
agree (up to natural isomorphism). Since
f:A\rightarrow L(A)
was carried to
\textnormal{id}_{L(A)}
(up to equivalence), it follows that the composition
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{L(A)\otimes_{A}(\cdot)}\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
is naturally isomorphic to
L^{\prime}:\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
. In particular,
L^{\prime}
admits a right adjoint
R^{\prime}:\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
given by the composition
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{(\cdot)|_{A}}\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})
. In order to prove our claim, it now suffices to show the following:

(i)

The map
R^{\prime}:\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is fully faithful.

(ii)

Let
\eta:\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
be the unit map which exhibits
L^{\prime}
as left adjoint to
R^{\prime}
. Then, the underlying
\mathcal{C}
-object of some
M\in\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is in
\mathcal{D}
precisely when
\eta_{M}:\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is an isomorphism.

We now prove these in order. Let
\gamma^{0}:\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})
be the counit which exhibits
L(A)\otimes_{A}(\cdot)
as left adjoint to
(\cdot)|_{A}
, and let
\gamma^{1}:\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
be the counit which exhibits the inclusion
i^{\prime}:\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})
as right adjoint to
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
. Let
\gamma^{0,\prime}
be the composition

\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\textnormal{id}\times i^{\prime}}\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\gamma^{0}}\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D}),

and let
\gamma:\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
be a composition
\gamma^{1}\circ\gamma^{0,\prime}
. Noting that
\gamma:\Delta^{1}\times\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
may be identified as a counit exhibiting
R^{\prime}
as right adjoint to
L^{\prime}
, in order to prove
(i)
it suffices to show that
\gamma
is a natural isomorphism (
[
10
]
02FF). Noting further that
\gamma^{1}
is a natural isomrphism, it suffices to show that
\gamma^{0,\prime}
is a natural isomorphism. In particular, it suffices to show that for each
N\in\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
, the map
\gamma^{0}_{N}:L(A)\otimes_{A}(i^{\prime}(N)|_{A})\rightarrow N
in
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})
is carried to an isomorphism by
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
. Since
5.1.3
implies that the map
i^{\prime}(N)|_{A}\rightarrow L(A)\otimes^{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(i^{\prime}(N)|_{A})
over
f:A\rightarrow L(A)
is taken to an isomorphism in
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
by
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
, the proof of
[
7
]
5.2.2.8 implies that it suffices to prove that the cartesian arrow
i^{\prime}(N)|_{A}\rightarrow i^{\prime}(N)
over
f:A\rightarrow L(A)
is taken to an isomorphism by
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{D})
, which follows immediately from
5.1.3
. We now show
(ii)
. Since the composition
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{R^{\prime}}\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
is canonically equivalent to the composition
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}
, we have that if the map
\eta_{M}:\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
of
(ii)
is an isomorphism then the underlying
\mathcal{C}
-object of
M
is in
\mathcal{D}
. Supposing now that the underlying
\mathcal{C}
-object of
M
is in
\mathcal{D}
, it suffices to show that the image of
\gamma_{M}
in
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
is an isomorphism (since
\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
is conservative). This follows immediately from the fact that
L^{\prime}:\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{LMod}^{{\mathbb{A}}_{\infty}}_{L(A)}(\mathcal{D})
is a reflective localization (by
(i)
), completing the proof of our claim.

\square

5.2
(Hyper)sheafification and Change of Universe

The main goal of this section is to show the following:

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
(\infty,1)
-category equipped with a Grothendieck topology
J
. Write
i:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
and
i^{\prime}:\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
for the corresponding inclusions, let
L:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
denote the sheafification functor, which we identify as an arrow
L:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
, and write
L^{\textnormal{hyp}}:\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{hyp}}
for the map left adjoint to the inclusion, which we identify as an arrow
L^{\textnormal{hyp}}:\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
. For each
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}
let
\tau_{\leq n}:\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n}\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
be the corresponding truncation functor, which we identify as an arrow
\tau_{\leq n}:\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
. Similarly define the maps
L^{\prime}:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
,
L^{\prime,\textnormal{hyp}}:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{hyp}}\subseteq\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
and
\tau^{\prime}_{\leq n}:\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\tau_{\leq n}\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
. Then:

(1)

i^{\prime}\circ L\cong L^{\prime}\circ i
as maps
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
.

(2)

i^{\prime}\circ\tau_{\leq n}\cong\tau^{\prime}_{\leq n}\circ i^{\prime}
as maps
\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
.
