(3)

i^{\prime}\circ L^{\textnormal{hyp}}\cong L^{\prime,\textnormal{hyp}}\circ i^{\prime}
as maps
\textnormal{Shv}_{J}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\widehat{\textnormal{Shv}}_{J}(\mathcal{C})
.

Remark 5.2.2
.

A proof of
5.2.1
(1) is given in Lurie’s
[
6
]
(see
[
6
]
2.4.10). At the time of writing this paper, the results
5.2.1
(2) and
5.2.1
(3) do not appear to be present in the literature.

Our proof will require some preliminaries.

Notation 5.2.3
.

Let
\Omega
be a strongly inaccessible cardinal, and let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal which is
\Omega
-small. We will use the following notation:

\bullet

An
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{D}
will be called
(\Omega,\kappa)
-accessible
if
\mathcal{D}
is
\kappa
-accessible with respect to the universe defined by
\Omega
.

\bullet

An
(\Omega,\kappa)
-accessible
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{D}
will be called an
(\Omega,\kappa)
-presentable
if
\mathcal{D}
admits
\Omega
-small colimits.

\bullet

For any essentially
\Omega
-small
(\infty,1)
-category
\mathcal{C}
, we will write
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Ind}_{\kappa}^{\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
for the
\textnormal{Ind}_{\kappa}
-completion of
\mathcal{C}
with respect to the universe defined by
\Omega
. More generally, we will call any
(\infty,1)
-functor with source
\mathcal{C}
isomorphic to
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Ind}_{\kappa}^{\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
an
(\Omega,\kappa)
-Ind completion
of
\mathcal{C}
.

\bullet

Let
\mathcal{D}
be an
(\infty,1)
-category which admits
\Omega
-small
\kappa
-filtered colimits. Then, we will call an object
X\in\mathcal{D}

(\Omega,\kappa)
-compact
if
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(X,-):\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega}
preserves
\Omega
-small
\kappa
-filtered colimits.

Lemma 5.2.4
.

Let
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime}
be strongly inaccessible cardinals satisfying
\Omega\leq\Omega^{\prime}
, and let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\Omega
-small
(\infty,1)
-category equipped with a Grothendieck topology
J
. Choose an
\Omega
-small uncountable regular cardinal
\kappa
such that
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
is
(\Omega,\kappa)
-accessible, and the inclusion
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})
preserves
\Omega
-small
\kappa
-filtered colimits. Write
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}
for the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
spanned by the
(\Omega,\kappa)
-compact objects, and similarly define
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}\subseteq\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
. Then:

(1)

The map
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
exhibits
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
as the
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-Ind completion of
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}
.

(2)

The inclusion
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
yields a categorical equivalence
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}
, where
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}\subseteq\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
denotes the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
spanned by the
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-compact objects.

Proof.

We first prove (1). To begin, we observe that since
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})
preserves
\Omega
-small
\kappa
-filtered colimits, the left adjoint
L:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
preserves
(\Omega,\kappa)
-compact objects (
[
7
]
5.5.7.2). It follows that the canonical map
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
factors as
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{<\Omega}^{J}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}_{<\Omega}^{J}(\mathcal{C})
. In particular, combining
[
10
]
06NW with
2.1.5
implies that for all
(\infty,1)
-categories
\mathcal{E}
which admit
\Omega
-small limits, the map
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\kappa}}((\textnormal{Shv}_{<\Omega}^{J}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{E})
is fully faithful, with essential image
\textnormal{Shv}_{\mathcal{E}}^{J}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{E})
. Let
\mathcal{D}
be an
(\infty,1)
-category which admits
\Omega^{\prime}
-small limits. Applying
2.1.5
, we deduce that the arrow
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega^{\prime}}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})
is fully faithful, with essential image
\textnormal{Shv}_{\mathcal{D}}^{J}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})
. Applying
5.2.5
, we observe that
\textnormal{Shv}_{<\Omega}^{J}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
preserves
\kappa
-small colimits. In particular, the map
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega^{\prime}}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})
factors as

\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega^{\prime}}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\kappa}}((\textnormal{Shv}_{<\Omega}^{J}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D}).

It follows that
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega^{\prime}}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\kappa}}((\textnormal{Shv}_{<\Omega}^{J}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})
is a categorical equivalence. Since
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega^{\prime}}}(\textnormal{Ind}_{\kappa}^{\Omega^{\prime}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\kappa}}((\textnormal{Shv}_{<\Omega}^{J}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{D})
is also an equivalence, the canonical map
\textnormal{Ind}_{\kappa}^{\Omega^{\prime}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}
right Kan extended from
(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}}
is an equivalence, proving (1). Claim (2) now follows from combining (1) with the fact that
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa}
is idempotent complete (
[
7
]
5.4.2.4).

,

\square

Lemma 5.2.5
.

Let
\Omega
be a strongly inaccessible cardinal, and let
\Omega^{\prime}
be a strongly inaccessible cardinal satisfying
\Omega^{\prime}\geq\Omega
. Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\Omega
-small
(\infty,1)
-category equipped with a Grothendieck topology
J
. Then, the inclusion
i:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
preserves all
\Omega
-small limits and all
\Omega
-small colimits.

Proof.

The piece of our claim concerning
\Omega
-small limits is immediate. The case of
\Omega
-small colimits may be deduced from
[
6
]
2.4.10, though to avoid circular reasoning in our proof of
5.2.1

(1)
we include here an alternative argument.
[
7
]
6.3.5.17 establishes the fact that the
(\infty,1)
-functor
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})
preserves
\Omega
-small colimits, and
2.1.5
implies that pulling-back along
\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}}
yields equivalences
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
and
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
. Our claim now follows from the fact that under these equivalences the inclusion
\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}^{\ell im_{<\Omega}}(\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})
may be identified as the inclusion
i:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
.

\square

Lemma 5.2.6
.

Fix a small regular cardinal
\kappa
, let
\mathcal{C}
be an
(\infty,1)
-category which admits small
\kappa
-filtered colimits, and let
\mathcal{D}
be a
\kappa
-accessible
(\infty,1)
-category which admits
\kappa
-small limits. Then, the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})_{\kappa}
of
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
spanned by maps
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
which preserve small
\kappa
-filtered colimits is closed under
\kappa
-small limits.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that, for all small
\kappa
-filtered
(\infty,1)
-categories
K
, the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(K^{\triangleright},\mathcal{D})
spanned by the colimit diagrams
F:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is closed under
\kappa
-small limits. Write
\mathcal{D}_{<\kappa}
for the full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}
spanned by the
\kappa
-compact objects, and let
K
be a small
\kappa
-filtered
(\infty,1)
-category. Since the fully faithful map
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}((\mathcal{D}_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
preserves small
\kappa
-filtered colimits and all existing limits, it suffices to prove that the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(K^{\triangleright},\textnormal{Fun}((\mathcal{D}_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}))
spanned by the colimit diagrams
F^{\prime}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}((\mathcal{D}_{<\kappa})^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S})
is closed under
\kappa
-small limits. This follows immediately from
[
10
]
05Y0.

\square

Lemma 5.2.7
.

Fix a small uncountable regular cardinal
\kappa
, and write
\textnormal{Pr}^{L,\kappa}
for the subcategory of
\textnormal{Pr}^{L}
spanned by those
\kappa
-presentable
(\infty,1)
-categories
\mathcal{C}
and morphisms
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
which preserve
\kappa
-compact objects. Write
\textnormal{Pr}^{R,\kappa}
for the subcategory of
\textnormal{Pr}^{R}
spanned by the
\kappa
-presentable
(\infty,1)
-categories and
\kappa
-accessible functors, and write
\widehat{\mathcal{C}at}_{\infty}
for the
(\infty,1)
-category of (possibly large)
(\infty,1)
-categories. Then,
\textnormal{Pr}^{L,\kappa}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{C}at}_{\infty}
is stable under
\kappa
-small limits and
\textnormal{Pr}^{R,\kappa}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{C}at}_{\infty}
is stable under small limits.

Proof.

The fact that
\textnormal{Pr}^{L,\kappa}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{C}at}_{\infty}
is stable under
\kappa
-small limits follows from combining
[
11
]
2.31 with
[
7
]
5.5.3.13, and the fact that
\textnormal{Pr}^{R,\kappa}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{C}at}_{\infty}
is stable under
\kappa
-small limits is precisely
[
7
]
5.5.7.6.

\square

The idea for the following proof is thanks to Jiacheng Liang:

Corollary 5.2.8
.

Under the notation of
5.2.4
, the inclusion
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
restricts to a map
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{hyp}}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{hyp}}
.

Proof.

Combining
5.2.4
with
[
10
]
06JY, we have that
\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
is
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-presentable, and the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))_{<\kappa}
spanned by the
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-compact objects may be identified as the essential image of
\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
. Writing
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(\infty)
for the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
spanned by the
\infty
-connective morphisms, we now wish to show the following:

(a)

\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(\infty)
is
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-accessible, and
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(\infty)\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
preserves
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-compact objects.

Since a map
f:X\rightarrow Y
is
\infty
-connective in
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
if and only if the image of
f
is
\infty
-connective in
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
(combine
5.2.5
with
[
7
]
6.5.1.18), (a) would immediately imply that for all
\infty
-connective morphisms
f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
in
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
there exists an
\Omega^{\prime}
-small
\kappa
-filtered
(\infty,1)
-category
K
and a diagram
F:K\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})\subseteq\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})
such that for each
k\in K
we have that
F(k)\in\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
, and that there exists a colimit diagram
\overline{F}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
exhibiting
f^{\prime}
as the colimit of the composition
K\xrightarrow{F}\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})_{<\kappa})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
. Thus, in order to prove our claim it suffices to show (a). We will deduce (a) from the following:

(b)

Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, and write
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(n)
for the full subcategory of
\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
spanned by the
n
-connective morphisms. Then,
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(n)
is
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-presentable, and the inclusion
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(n)\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}))
preserves
\Omega^{\prime}
-small colimits and
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-compact objects.

Temporarily assuming (b), write
U:{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{\textnormal{opp}}\rightarrow\textnormal{Pr}^{L,\Omega^{\prime}}_{\kappa}
for the diagram

\cdots\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(2)\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(1)\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(0),

. We may now combine (b) and

is

-accessible, and that the inclusion

preserves
(\Omega^{\prime},\kappa)
-compact objects, proving (a). We now show (b). Let

, and write
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(\tau,n)
for the full subcategory of

for the map left adjoint to the inclusion (
[

]

along

]

-compact objects.

Noting that
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(\tau,n)
and the map

are well-defined for

(

]

. For each natural transformation

Proof.

(
5.2.1
.) We begin by proving
(1)
. First, write
T
for the isomorphism class of monomorphisms of the form
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})
, where
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
m:S\rightarrow h^{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
corresponds to a
J
-sieve on
X
under the bijection of
[
7
]
6.2.2.5. Noting that the composition
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{L^{\prime}}\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
preserve small colimits and carries each arrow in
T
to an isomorphism, it follows from
2.1.4
that
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
carries each
L
-equivalence to an isomorphism. Thus,
i:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})
carries
L
-equivalences to
L^{\prime}
-equivalences. Next, write
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})^{0}
for the essential image of
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})
in
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})
. Since
i:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})
restricts to the map
i^{\prime}:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
, it immediately follows that
L^{\prime}:\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})
restricts to a map
\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})^{0}\rightarrow\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{opp}},\mathcal{S}_{<\Omega^{\prime}})^{0}
, proving (1).

Claim (2) may be deduced from (1), though here we present an alternative argument. Let
n\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq-2}
. To begin, observe that
i^{\prime}:\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
carries
n
-truncated objects to
n
-truncated objects. So, just as in the proof of (1), it suffices to prove that
i^{\prime}
carries
\tau_{\leq n}
-equivalences to
\tau^{\prime}_{\leq n}
-equivalences. However, it follows from
5.2.5
that
i^{\prime}
is a map of
\infty
-pretopoi in the sense of
[
9
]
A.6.4.1, so our claim follows from
[
9
]
A.6.7.1.

We now show (3). Combining
5.2.5
with
[
7
]
6.5.1.18, we have that
i^{\prime}
carries
L^{\textnormal{hyp}}
-equivalences to
L^{\prime,\textnormal{hyp}}
-equivalences. Thus, just as in the proof of (1) (and the proof of (2)), it suffices to show that
i^{\prime}
carries
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{hyp}}\subseteq\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega}(\mathcal{C})
to
\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{hyp}}\subseteq\textnormal{Shv}^{J}_{<\Omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
, which we have shown in
5.2.8
.

\square
